Audited against the cached text of Gregory Morse's 2018 paper, DOI
10.24425/119359, and the unchanged repository sources on 2026-10-05.
The source locators are listing numbers, which remain stable across PDF layouts.
This inventory records coverage and differences; an inventory entry does not
certify an unproved printed macro.

## Source artifacts

- `../smcTuring/main.cpp` and `../smcTuring/tm.asm` are the original comparison
  implementations. The C++ entry points build finite transition/tape data and
  call the external MASM simulators. `MovTM` uses ordinary MOV loads into
  registers, so these files alone are not the paper's strict pure-SMC witness.
  Their before/after tests and unchanged hashes are in `../validation/baseline.json`
  and `baseline-after-repair.json`.
- `../validation/.cache/morse-2018.txt` is the published listing inventory.
  Its extracted typography contains wrapped expressions and ambiguous aliases;
  the PDF remains the authority when resolving a literal instruction encoding.
- `../smcTuring/pure_smc.inc` contains the original and corrected DEFADDR
  regression primitives. `../validation/generate_pure.py` constructs the
  disclosed shared-reader backend; it is not a transcription of Listing 8.
- The literal source of the foundation is **Stephen Dolan's** 2013
  *mov is Turing-complete*. Its normalizations are D01-D05 in `ERRATA.md`.

## Listing inventory

| Published location | Operation or claim | Checked correspondence and remaining literal work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Listing 1 | Three logical MOV forms: immediate assignment, indexed load and indexed store | `Dolan/Mov` specifies their tagged word effects. `SMC/ConcreteCompiler` and `Native/SourceReadiness`/`ProgramCertificate` cover the adapted backend, including register aliases and invalid address tags. Printed inline templates are not claimed to be byte-identical to that backend. |
| Listing 2: MOFFSMOV | Full-width accumulator store with a mutable absolute address operand | `Native/Encoding`, `ByteMemory` and `Fetch` prove exact selected x86/x64 encoding and current-operand fetch. x64 uses REX.W and an eight-byte address. |
| Listing 2: DEFADDR | Address placeholder and dereference with final memory restoration | M01 identifies the register-only-load purity defect. `Defaddr` proves the exchange repair, boundary equivalence and exact self-aliasing. Native reader proofs establish the adapted byte execution. |
| Listing 2: DEFDATA32 | A data word stored in an instruction operand | E146-E147 prove the printed ten-byte DWORD self-store in both modes: initial zero, arbitrary live operand patches/current-byte fetch/full AX/SP/CF/whole memory/code writing/reuse. Complete native packets and 6,400 executions per mode pass. M14 uses that existing owner for the scalar in Listing 8. The constructed storage-owner grid is proved in `SMC/ConcreteImage` and `Native/GridImage`/`SpacedCombinedImage`. The native backend uses full-width MOFFS owners rather than the printed immediate-store owner. |
| Listing 2: DATAOP | A unary or binary operation on an operand field | Purity requires an operation that actually writes memory and a code-owned destination. The selected SUB/SBB/ADD/XOR/AND/MOV forms are proved; the parameter is not an unrestricted instruction whitelist. |
| Listing 2: OP64 | Two 32-bit operand operations in long mode | Y02 (`X64/HalfWords`) checks the exact value algebra: corresponding-half Boolean operations and assignment lift to 64 bits; immediate OP64 repeats its 32-bit operand. ADD/SUB require the checked carry/borrow correction or no-carry/no-borrow premise. M04 records the corrected split-word contract and its positive justification. Literal field locations and frames remain S01/A02/E05; the adapted backend uses full-width operations. |
| Listing 2: MOVEMODEOFFS | Patching a displacement or address field | `Native/Relocation`, `LayoutPass` and `CompiledLayout` prove the adapted computed coordinates, x86 absolute addressing, x64 RIP-relative addressing and signed rel32 bounds. Literal printed expression transcription remains separate. |
| Listing 2: DOHALT | Code-writing terminal marker | M12 changes the zero store to its own DWORD immediate at IMMOFFS and observes termination immediately afterwards. E136-E138 prove current/original-image fetch, endpoint, AX/SP/CF, whole-memory/output preservation, code-owned writing and reusable encoding in both modes. Each architecture passes 4,128 typed-original and 4,128 corrected cases; complete loaded/COFF packets match Lean. Literal Listing 8/9 composition and physical unexpected-fault/fetch remain P07/A02/E05. See APPENDIX_TERMINAL.md. |
| Listing 3: COMPARE | Subtract/negate/borrow-mask comparison with optional restoration | Borrow/mask and exact tag comparisons are checked in `SMC/Control`, `Native/Arithmetic` and the complete guard proofs. The printed optional NEG/preservation cases and general initial mask contract remain part of E01/E02. A zero-initialized SBB destination is explicit in the checked guard. |

That unprovided implementation has no literal proof here. Resource/performance
claims are empirical and are separate from computation correctness.

## Outcome and resource contracts

| Layer | Accepted progress | Intended termination | Other limits or failures |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Finite mathematical TM | A defined transition produces the next full configuration | An undefined transition is the intended halt, retaining the entire tape and head | Its tape model is unbounded; finite alphabet/control and finite input are explicit. |
| Dolan tagged-word simulator | A finite candidate scan resolves each TM transition with positive progress | The disclosed sentinel guard produces the terminal source outcome | Malformed tags are separate source traps. Initialization and simulation invariants exclude them on valid TM runs. |
| Constructed unbounded pure SMC | Actual current-memory fetch, source effects and a code-backed CALL backedge | Arrival at the designated terminal corresponds to the source halt and preserves recoverable output | This is an unbounded word model. Purity means every reached computational instruction writes an owned instruction field; actual changes/unbounded distinct fields have separate proofs. |
| Selected native byte model | Capacity/compactness/source-address bounds derive the full actual kernel trace | A pass stops at its calculated continuation or intended source-trap target | `Execution` is a selected nonfaulting PC/AX/SP/CF projection. It does not model every possible architectural fault. |
| Finite loaded native evaluator | Live-byte fetch supplies the form; all header, packet, data-read and full-word-write windows must be contained | The proved finite trace has the same final observed state and every represented finite source word | A rejected step is a partial-evaluator failure, not automatically an intended TM halt. N23-N27 construct finite loaded execution for each actual native prefix. N28-N35 place reader/scalar/read-update fragments in the preselected allocation; N36-N42 compose both complete guards and their actual callers. N43-N55 close dynamic source and full kernel/backedge containment in the allocation fixed before execution; physical hardware conditions remain separate. |
| Checked mapped native evaluator | N56-N58 apply fixed canonical-address endpoints and effective RWX permission bits to the actual kernel/backedge | The same source outcome, full finite output, owned writes and closed entry PC are retained | Live-byte decode and all header/packet/read/full-word-write checks pass under one static environment certificate. Invalid PC/missing execute permission is a rejection, not a source halt. Physical mapping/configuration and instruction-fetch coherence remain separate. |
| Combined checked native evaluator | N61-N64 derive both accepted and rejected endpoints and check all memory/permission/CALL-target conditions together | Exact source outcome, every finite source word, code-owned writes and literal final PC are preserved | `terminalOffset < hostSpan` derives the unfetched terminal target from initialized frozen metadata; MASM checks the actual host geometry. Bad CALL targets are evaluator failures, not source halts. Actual mapping/configuration/ISA/fetch coherence remains external. |
| Continuous repeated native passes | N65-N67 thread actual byte memory through successive literal source runs, accepted backedges and first rejection | One finite checked trace retains full represented output, code ownership, all modeled checks and exact final PC | The original snapshot/invariant is maintained throughout; source-path bounds and static mapping/host conditions are explicit. At least three events occur per accepted pass. The initializer is called only once. Physical ISA/configuration/fetch remains external. |
| Physical x86/x64 realization | N55 proves the modeled access extent is contained in the prescribed allocation; that allocation must satisfy the declared physical execution conditions | Host observation detects the designated endpoint and decodes represented output | Canonical/segment/permission/CET/current-fetch conditions must hold. Exhausting physical resources or suffering a hardware fault is not identified with source-machine halting. |

Finite loaded state is now constructed rather than postulated. N23-N27
initially computed a containing interval from successive actual accesses. N43-N55 now prove that every full kernel/backedge access lies inside
the allocation fixed by emitted layout and the requested source-grid capacity.
It is a containing interval, not a claim that every byte in it is accessed. The proof does
not establish that an arbitrary such natural-address interval is mapped or
canonical on hardware; that remains a distinct deployment obligation.

The repository errata and the checked adapted construction provide a remedy for
DEFADDR's local purity defect. They do not turn unproved supplementary algorithms
or the original printed interpreter into a literal-source proof.

prefix from one initial image, entry and extent bound: actual x/y words, seven
real owners, own imm32 identities, protected constants/headers, field layout,
every fetched form/next PC and all-relation register/word/CF/exterior-byte results.

operand coordinates against twelve Lean emitter exports. E20-E24 close the complete reference byte-tail/return-alias/continuation
execution and PCs. General host-spacing and physical ISA/fetch remain open. See [APPENDIX_BYTE_IMAGE.md](APPENDIX_BYTE_IMAGE.md).

preserved. SP restoration and initializer-code reuse require execution of the
printed target continuation next; neither is asserted at this CALL endpoint.
